### Keep your eye on the tank: A fresh look at an accelerating frame demonstration
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33049
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33049v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A water tank sliding down a frictionless ramp is a classic example of an accelerated reference frame. Along with other accelerated frames, Bartlett analyzed frictionless sliding, and showed that the liquid's surface is parallel to the surface of the ramp, rather than the base. Duzen and coworkers used liquid in an accelerating tank to help students explore motion, noting that the ramp case and riding on a cart accelerated by a hanging mass are equivalent. Munera used the second as a lecture demonstration. In both Refs. 2 and 3, students viewed accelerating tanks from the lab frame, which may be hard to observe at a distance. As the goal of these demonstrations is to understand the effects of the acceleration in the tank frame, tank-frame observations would be beneficial. Following Krulj and Slisko, a camera was mounted to an accelerated object to make in-frame videos of liquid-based equivalence principle demonstrations. Previous work did not quantify the actual acceleration of the tank; use of a Smart Cart simplifies this measurement.

### Wave propagation analysis for a second strain gradient rod theory
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33083
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33083v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: In this work, an enriched model describing the longitudinal wave propagation is established based on Mindlin's Second Strain Gradient (SSG) theory, which can describe the heterogeneity caused by the micro-structure interactions in the frame of continuum mechanics. The governing equation and associated boundary conditions are derived based on Hamilton's principle, then the dispersion relation of non-classical longitudinal wave together with the extra-waves appearing exclusively in SSG theory model are investigated. The investigations are based on the modal density, energy flow, and forced response of the rod. Wave transmission and reflection through planar interfaces based on the proposed model have been calculated. Finally, the results of the enriched model are well interpreted by comparing with the classical theory results, and some useful conclusions are derived on the SSG theory based model in the wave propagation characterization.

### Exceptional points and cusp singularities in relativistic stellar oscillations with causal bulk relaxation
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33089
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33089v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Causal relativistic hydrodynamics introduces relaxation degrees of freedom that elevate a quadratic oscillation problem to a cubic non-Hermitian spectral pencil. We study its exceptional-point (EP2) curves and an $A_3$ cusp, and construct an exactly solvable radiating wave--relaxation problem with a P\"oschl--Teller potential and an Israel--Stewart channel. Using hyperboloidal coordinates, outgoing Siegert conditions become endpoint conditions. For the aligned transport profile and away from discrete resonant couplings, we prove that the complete quasinormal point spectrum decomposes into a tower of sector cubics, with radiative damping rates determined by the boundary-value problem. The resulting EP2 and cusp geometry is therefore realized by genuine quasinormal eigenvalues. Multiprecision bordered-Newton calculations certify the degeneracies and their square-root and cubic-root splitting laws.

### An Efficient Quantum Spectral-Galerkin Method for Linear Elliptic Equations
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33095
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33095v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: This paper presents an efficient quantum spectral-Galerkin method for constant-coefficient second-order linear elliptic equations. The method employs a boundary-adapted Jacobi tensor-product basis that incorporates the boundary condition into the approximation space and produces Galerkin systems with polynomially bounded condition numbers and sparsity. For analytic solutions satisfying a suitable uniform regularity condition, spectral convergence allows the normalized Galerkin coefficient state to be prepared with complexity $\mathrm{poly}(d,\log(1/\epsilon))$, where $d$ is the spatial dimension and $\epsilon$ is the error tolerance, under standard sparse-access and state-preparation assumptions. At the state-preparation level, this improves the precision dependence over fixed-order finite-difference and finite-element discretizations. We further show how to estimate prescribed linear functionals of the solution, with an additional $O(1/\epsilon)$ factor arising from norm and inner-product estimation.

### A Universal Constitutive Relation for Static Droplet Retention
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33100
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33100v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Under applied forcing, static droplet retention is accompanied by contact angle asymmetry, with unequal apparent contact angles on opposite sides. Yet, the relation between the asymmetry and forcing lacks a common constitutive basis. Here, we show that the asymmetry emerges from minimizing the forcing-dependent free energy. The response of the asymmetry to forcing defines a susceptibility whose zero-forcing value yields a universal constitutive relation between the asymmetry and forcing. The constitutive relation is quantitatively validated across liquids, volumes, and forcing levels.

### Spatial symmetry invariance of three-dimensional Navier-Stokes turbulence
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33115
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.33115v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Direct Numerical Simulation (DNS) of the deterministic Navier-Stokes (NS) equations is widely used to provide benchmark solutions of turbulent flow, but its accuracy and reliability have not been rigorously inspected from a mathematical viewpoint. Here we prove a spatial symmetry invariance theorem (SSIT) of three-dimensional NS equations whereby an exact solution of deterministic NS equations under periodic boundary conditions maintains the same spatial symmetry as its symmetric initial flow, forever. SSIT provides an absolute benchmark test for computational fluid dynamics (CFD). It is shown herein that traditional DNS of NS turbulence quickly violates SSIT, revealing that the spatiotemporal trajectory of NS turbulence predicted by DNS soon becomes badly polluted by numerical noise. Importantly, it is found that even the statistical results of DNS might be unstable, i.e., exhibit sensitivity dependence on the initial flow conditions.

### A Deep-Mantle Garnet Might Hold the Secret to Ancient Martian Magma
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-27 12:38 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/a-deep-mantle-garnet-might-hold-the-secret-to-ancient-martian-magma
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A history of a planet’s volcanic activity is contained in its rocks. And Mars’ rocks - or at least many of the meteorites that have made the journey from it to Earth - were telling a contradictory story. They appeared to be highly oxidized, but our understanding of the internal volcanic forces on Mars meant that they should have been formed in a reducing environment - the exact opposite of what the chemical makeup of the rocks themselves indicate. Now, a new paper published in the Journal of Geophysical Research: Solid Earth from planetary scientists Hideharu Kuwahara and Ryoichi Nakada of Ehime University provides an answer as to why - and it involves a gemstone.

The Earth’s magnetic field is responsible for shielding us from harmful space weather, including solar radiation from the Sun but also cosmic rays originating from outside our solar system. Cosmic rays can include deep space supernovas, so the magnetic field takes the majority of the brunt from solar radiation. Without our magnetic field, the solar wind would slowly strip away our atmosphere, making life on our small, blue world impossible to exist. But where is the magnetic field doing the heaviest lifting regarding how it filters out incoming space radiation?

Kaushik Bhattacharya is an engineer and materials scientist, but he has always been fascinated by seashells. He marvels at nature's engineering capabilities.

Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-20 23:40 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-shipwrecks-reefs-hidden-toxic.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Late last year, scientists in Germany published findings on a World War II munitions dump in the Baltic Sea. Some may dismiss the dump as hazardous waste, but to marine biologists it was something far more exciting: a thriving ecosystem.

### Octopus and cuttlefish suckers may have evolved by repurposing ancient developmental genes
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-20 13:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-octopus-cuttlefish-suckers-evolved-repurposing.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Cephalopods, like the octopus, have specialized arms and suckers, but how these structures evolved from the simple bodies of their mollusk ancestors remains a mystery. Researchers from the University of Tokyo compared gene activity during embryonic development of cuttlefish, octopus and snail body parts. They found that several genes associated with appendage patterning showed expression along developing cephalopod arms, but no comparable patterns in developing suckers. Developing suckers instead expressed Ets1, a gene associated with early embryonic development in mollusks. The findings suggest cephalopods evolved suckers by reusing existing genes in new ways.

### Oldest animals on Earth found to share a common wormlike ancestor
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-19 17:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-oldest-animals-earth-common-wormlike.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

New research from the University of St Andrews has shed light on the evolution of an important yet thus far poorly understood group of marine invertebrate animals—brachiopods. The work, published in Science Advances, presents the first comprehensive phylogenomic analysis of all five extant brachiopod orders and related lineages.

### Mollusk family trees play a key role in shaping microbiomes, researchers find
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-14 20:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-mollusk-family-trees-play-key.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A mollusk's microbial community has more to do with its family tree than its address. In other words, the composition of a mollusk's microbiome depends less on the environment in which it lives and more on what species it belongs to, say University of Michigan researchers.

I like writing Javascript without a build system and for the millionth time yesterday I ran into a problem where I needed to figure out how to import a Javascript library in my code without using a build system, and it took FOREVER to figure out how to import it because the library’s setup instructions assume that you’re using a build system. ### Why pipes sometimes get "stuck": buffering
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2024/11/29/why-pipes-get-stuck-buffering/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Here’s a niche terminal problem that has bothered me for years but that I never really understood until a few weeks ago. Let’s say you’re running this command to watch for some specific output in a log file: tail -f /some/log/file | grep thing1 | grep thing2 If log lines are being added to the file relatively slowly, the result I’d see is… nothing! It doesn’t matter if there were matches in the log file or not, there just wouldn’t be any output. I internalized this as “uh, I guess pipes just get stuck sometimes and don’t show me the output, that’s weird”, and I’d handle it by just running grep thing1 /some/log/file | grep thing2 instead, which would work. So as I’ve been doing a terminal deep dive over the last few months I was really excited to finally learn exactly why this happens. why this happens: buffering The reason why “pipes get stuck” sometimes is that it’s VERY common for programs to buffer their output before writing it to a pipe or file.

### "Rules" that terminal programs follow
Source: Julia Evans
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://jvns.ca/blog/2024/11/26/terminal-rules/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Recently I’ve been thinking about how everything that happens in the terminal is some combination of: Your operating system ’s job Your shell ’s job Your terminal emulator ’s job The job of whatever program you happen to be running (like top or vim or cat ) The first three (your operating system, shell, and terminal emulator) are all kind of known quantities – if you’re using bash in GNOME Terminal on Linux, you can more or less reason about how how all of those things interact, and some of their behaviour is standardized by POSIX. But the fourth one (“whatever program you happen to be running”) feels like it could do ANYTHING. How are you supposed to know how a program is going to behave?

Hello! Recently I ran a terminal survey and I asked people what frustrated them. One person commented: There are so many pieces to having a modern terminal experience. I wish it all came out of the box. My immediate reaction was “oh, getting a modern terminal experience isn’t that hard, you just need to….”, but the more I thought about it, the longer the “you just need to…” list got, and I kept thinking about more and more caveats. So I thought I would write down some notes about what it means to me personally to have a “modern” terminal experience and what I think can make it hard for people to get there. what is a “modern terminal experience”? I was talking to a friend about how to add a directory to your PATH today. It’s something that feels “obvious” to me since I’ve been using the terminal for a long time, but when I searched for instructions for how to do it, I actually couldn’t find something that explained all of the steps – a lot of them just said “add this to ~/.bashrc ”, but what if you’re not using bash? What if your bash config is actually in a different file? And how are you supposed to figure out which directory to add anyway? ### The Conductor Developer
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings/conductor-developer.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

TL;DR Why I think software development is starting to feel a little more like conducting an orchestra. There’s a shift happening in software development that I don’t think we’re talking about clearly enough. For the last couple of years we’ve framed AI as a productivity tool. How much faster can it write code? How many more features can we ship? How much cheaper can we build software? I think that’s the wrong question, but I understand why. The first thing AI became good at was writing code, so naturally that’s where we focused. As AI got better at coding, I expected the bottlenecks to move through the software delivery lifecycle: from coding to design and specification, architecture, then verification. And they have. We spent a lot of time at the most recent FOSE event discussing how we ensure good design, quality and resilience while agents increasingly write the code. That’s a topic for another ramble. A few months ago, though, I realised I was looking at the wrong bottleneck. I kept assuming it would simply move to the next phase of software delivery. I was wrong.

### Fragments: August 4
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-08-04.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

There’s been a fair bit of publicity of the Open AI “rogue agent” that hacked into Hugging Face . This prompted Anthropic to check what their models were up to and, to my complete lack of surprise, discovered three incidents where models had gained unauthorized access to data in other organizations. Simon Wilison concluded : It’s abundantly clear now that running evals of cyberattack potential in models is a spectacularly risky business. Every AI lab needs to pay attention to this. Keeping a close eye on what’s happening in those sandboxes is crucial It strikes me that this is akin to a virus escaping from a laboratory. It makes clear that the model builders are not putting sufficient controls in place to prevent these lab escapes. They are morally responsible for any consequences of this, and that should extend to legal liability too. The bigger concern however is that this same kind of thing can happen with any organization running open-weight models. Lots of labs playing around with dangerous tools and little idea how to contain them.

### Fragments: August 18
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-08-18.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Part of the reason why I’m at Thoughtworks is because I’d like to see a software development organization founded on technical excellence as an example for the rest of the industry. The trouble is that I have little aptitude or inclination for the hard work of building such an organization. So I rely on working with people who are prepared to actually put the effort in. A key partner in all of this is Rachel Laycock , who is the global CTO of Thoughtworks. Not just is she far better than me at running a technology organization, she’s also a keen observer and connector of ideas. I’ve been urging her to write these down, even if her busy schedule makes it difficult for her to compose them into something substantial. Happily she’s starting writing “Rachel’s Ramblings” Fast, imperfect, thinking out loud. Naming ideas early rather than waiting until they’re fully formed. Because the reality is, most of what I do day to day isn’t answering known questions. It’s spotting patterns and asking questions we haven’t quite figured out yet.

### 333 Tor Exit Nodes Hijacked To Intercept SSH Traffic And Retrieve Plaintext Email
Source: SoylentNews
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://soylentnews.org/breakingnews/article.pl?sid=26/10/22/1248234&from=rss
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

https://arstechnica.com/information-technology/2026/10/300-tor-exit-nodes-hijacked-to-intercept-ssh-traffic-and-retrieve-plaintext-email/ Unknown hackers hijacked more than 300 Tor exit nodes and reconfigured them to favor unencrypted email traffic and redirect SSH connections through servers they controlled, a researcher reported Tuesday. The hijacking began in late August, when according to Tor volunteers , the attacker used a valid password that had been reused to gain access to a Tor management server. The attacker then installed Ebury , a backdoor written for Linux that was discovered 12 years ago. Then the attacker connected from the management server to a second Tor server, called Switzerland2, through SSH, using an RSA key that, for unknown reasons, investigators couldn't find among Switzerland2's authorized keys. With administrative control of Switzerland2, the attackers installed Ebury there as well. Finally, the attacker reconfigured settings on two exit nodes that Switzerland2 hosted.

Arthur T Knackerbracket writes: https://arstechnica.com/tech-policy/2026/10/comcast-loses-more-customers-claims-other-isps-cheap-prices-are-irrational/ Comcast reportedly plans significant job cuts in its cable division as the firm continues to lose residential broadband and TV customers. Comcast lost 144,000 residential broadband customers in Q3 2026, up from a 91,000-customer loss in the same quarter a year ago, the company said in its earnings report today. Comcast historically capitalized on a lack of competition for high-speed wired broadband. But now it faces stronger competition from fixed wireless providers and from fiber providers in parts of its cable territory. "Fixed wireless remains aggressive and continues to take share, while fiber construction remains elevated, with pockets of irrational pricing and promotional activity," Comcast co-CEO Mike Cavanagh said today in an earnings call . Comcast CFO Jason Armstrong said "irrational pricing" from fiber operators includes $30 to $40 monthly broadband offers "in specific markets in the third quarter.

### Quoting Geoffrey Huntley
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-27 19:47 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/13/geoffrey-huntley/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

If the agent isn't using my diagnostics library, I could introspect and update AGENTS.md, but there's still a delta, and it'll drift. Instead, I write a pre-commit hook that checks the git diff for direct calls to observability modules that bypass my diagnostics module. Boom: fail the commit, inject a prompt telling the agent to use the diagnostics library. All of a sudden it's ten out of ten. — Geoffrey Huntley , nobody should type code by hand anymore. here's what's left. ### llm-gemini 0.35
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-27 19:47 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/13/llm-gemini/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Release: llm-gemini 0.35 I added support to llm-gemini for the new Gemini 4 Argon model, made available today via the Gemini API and for customers of their $100/month subscription plan. Here are the low, medium and high pelicans . As expected, they are all excellent: Gemini has a track record of producing great SVGs, and are the only lab where I know their staff have invested in SVG specifically (and who have boasted about their pelicans before.) Here's the "high" illustration, which cost me 17.166 cents: The pricing is interesting. At launch, Argon is $2/million input and $10/million output - the same price as GPT-6 Sol, half the price of Claude Opus 5.5 and 1/5th the price of Claude Fable 5.1 and GPT-6 Astra. But on December 1st Argon is set to double in price - so it will be the same price as Opus 5.5. Prices like this aren't really comparable these days since what really matters is how many tokens a model takes to achieve a goal within an agentic harness. One useful clue for that comes from Artificial Analysis, who publish the total cost to run their benchmark using each model.

### Redesigning source control at Anthropic
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-27 19:47 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/13/redesigning-source-control-at-anthropic/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Redesigning source control at Anthropic Interesting piece from Anthropic describing why and how they built their own custom alternative to Git, based on the dramatic increase in rate of changes to their code after the past six months. In May 2026, Anthropic averaged around 2,500 code changes merged per day; as of September, we merged over 30,000 per day. They included a chart showing those changes. I had Claude modify their chart ( code here ) to add markers for the public releases of each of their models in 2026: This shows public release dates, not the date when models became available for Anthropic's internal use. My guess is that the introduction of Fable/Mythos class models is a key factor in the increased coding velocity. "Antique" is their new code review system. The source control system is called Scion, and sounds to me like a reinvention of centralized source control along the lines of Subversion.

AI is mass decompiling games. That's both awesome and scary. Indie game developer Popcar talks about the recent wave of AI-powered decompilation of existing games, enabling weird mashups between existing games, and producing a boom in game preservation projects to get old games working on new hardware (often in a browser using WebAssembly). I like how this piece captures both the positives and the negatives of this new development: Most people already realized the scary part by now. If AI can decompile games and software, then the entire tech industry is about to explode. Anybody can decompile other software and use it any way they'd like, and nobody can stop them. Companies can steal from competitors. Your paid game or app can be open-sourced against your will. This isn't exclusive to old video games, we can apply this to any software. Tool: Tree Appreciator Another game experiment with Claude. This one used the default Claude iPhone app (chat mode, not Claude Code mode), where I prompted Claude Opus 5.5 Max with: Build a three js game called "Tree appreciator" - it shows you two algorithmically generated trees and the game mechanics is about appreciating the differences between them. Think hard about how to make sure it is both fun and beautiful. Somewhat embarrassingly given my recent talk , I've found that telling Opus 5.5 "make sure it is fun" works uncomfortably well. In this case Claude crunched for 3 hours and 7 minutes before retuning a cosy little spot-the-difference game with music and animation and achievements that's quite compelling to play. ### LaTeX renderer
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-27 19:47 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/15/latex/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Tool: LaTeX renderer Inspired by Paul Ford and Aboard Foundary's new web app Tiny Little Office , which provides a suite of web app content editors (a Word Processor, Outliner, Presentation Builder, Spreadsheet, Calculator, Drawing Tool, Diagram Maker, Animator, Calendar, and Trip Planner). Tiny Little Office's signature trick is to store all of the document data in the #fragment section of the URL, so every document created using it becomes a bookmarkable and shareable link. It also has an llms.txt file telling agents how to construct URLs that will render as documents. Claude used that file to generate this link to a deck that explains how the URL format works: Tiny Little Office inspired me to have Claude Code (Opus 5.5 Extra) build a similar tool that works with LaTeX. Here's the sequence of prompts I used: I want a tool where you can link to it with a fragment hash #code-here and it treats that code as LaTeX and renders it really effectively. It should also support a prefix which means the latex is compressed with a custom compression dictionary optimized for latex.

### Robinson: Raspberry Pi 5 on Fedora
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-27 18:11 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1103212/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Peter Robinson has written a blog post about the current state of Fedora support for Raspberry Pi 5 devices. According to Robinson, the upcoming Fedora 45 release will work—with caveats—on all variants of the Raspberry Pi Model 5B; the Compute Module 5 and Pi 500+ are still works in progress. For Fedora 44 release you need to have a temporary workaround when booting. If you update to the latest kernel/firmware straight away this isn't then needed beyond the first boot. The workaround isn't needed for Fedora 45 so if you're going to try this out I suggest you go and grab your favourite flavour of Fedora from the Fedora 45 release candidates and jump straight in there! You will get an accelerated desktop with storage/network/USB. There's not currently any support for sound and a number of other things. See Robinson's post for instructions on creating an Fedora image to use with a Raspberry Pi 5.

### [$] Python's experimental JIT to be removed
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-26 15:45 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1102102/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Python's experimental just-in-time (JIT) compiler , which was added to CPython in 2024 for the 3.13 release , is on its way out. In June, the Python steering council announced that it was pausing work on the JIT in CPython's main branch until there was an accepted Python Enhancement Proposal (PEP) that addressed concerns about maintenance, compatibility, and set clear success metrics as well as a timeline for completion. After creating PEP 836 ("JIT Go Brrr: The Path to a Supported JIT Compiler for CPython") to try to meet the council's request, and after months of discussion, the JIT maintainers have withdrawn the PEP. For now, it appears that development on the once-promising project has come to an end.

### Systemd adds support for components written in Rust
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-26 15:13 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1102970/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Christian Brauner has announced that he has merged support for writing leaf components of systemd in Rust. Rust will be used only for new leaf components, existing code is not being ported to Rust and nothing that other components depend on is written in Rust. Brauner said that the project would like to move forward with Rust support in core systemd, " but there are several blockers in Rust's support for dynamic libraries which prevents us from doing that without massively bloating every binary we build ". Assuming all goes well, it seems likely this work will appear in the systemd v263 release expected in December.

### Powering the agentic enterprise with latest innovations in Oracle AI Database@Google Cloud
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 19:30 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/databases/oracle-ai-databasegoogle-cloud-whats-new-at-oracle-ai-world/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

For decades, core enterprise operations — from global supply chains and financial ledgers to billing engines — have relied on the performance and transactional integrity of Oracle databases. Today at Oracle AI World in Las Vegas, Google Cloud and Oracle are expanding our partnership across the AI stack — spanning AI compute and infrastructure (including making Google TPUs available through Oracle Cloud Infrastructure), enterprise data, and agentic AI — by deepening the integration between Oracle AI Database@Google Cloud, Google’s Agentic Data Cloud , and Gemini Enterprise. By unifying Oracle’s mission-critical operational data and applications with Google Cloud’s data and AI ecosystem — including BigQuery, Gemini Enterprise, and Gemini models — enterprises can move beyond static analytics and build autonomous, agentic workflows. This architecture breaks down traditional data silos, giving agents direct, secure, and real-time access to the foundational enterprise data required to drive real-world action.

### Securely connecting Gemini Enterprise to Snowflake data
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/data-analytics/bridge-gemini-enterprise-and-snowflake-with-google-adk/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Enterprise AI needs to go beyond simple chat interfaces. To provide real value, AI agents must access your data warehouses to generate actionable insights, all while maintaining strict security and data governance. This guide explores how to build a secure bridge between Google Cloud’s Gemini Enterprise and Snowflake. By using the Model Context Protocol (MCP) and the Google Agent Development Kit (ADK), you can empower your agent to act as an assistant that understands natural language, generates precise SQL via Snowflake Cortex and executes queries with user-level permissions. Choosing the right integration pattern Direct Connection vs. custom orchestration The Google ADK architecture demonstrated in this post represents the advanced / custom orchestration pattern. It is designed for developers building complex, multi-agent systems on Google Cloud, such as workflows that combine Snowflake insights with external enterprise tools, custom reasoning loops, or cross-platform state management in Gemini Agent Engine.

### Scale agents securely with IAM Helper Agent Skills and MCP Tools
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/scale-agents-securely-with-iam-helper-agent-skills-and-mcp-tools/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

As organizations adopt autonomous agents to build applications, securing identities and access has become more challenging. Developers relying on agents to provision cloud resources, write Terraform, and deploy microservices at machine-speed only find their CI/CD pipelines blo cked even faster as security friction scales. To help you get ahead of agentic-era Identity and Access Management, today we’re introducing IAM Helper. This suite of intelligent, agent-facing building blocks can automate complex, end-to-end IAM workflows. As an agent-native orchestration and interface layer for Google Cloud IAM, it augments Google Cloud’s core IAM engines — including Policy Intelligence and Privileged Access Manager (PAM) — making IAM skills callable directly by autonomous code assistants, deployment pipelines, and custom agents. Using this functionality reduces the administrative overhead of maintaining least-privilege security and agent governance, while maximizing utility, and accelerating deployment.

### Bringing Google TPUs to Oracle Cloud Infrastructure
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 14:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/infrastructure/bringing-google-tpus-to-oracle-cloud-infrastructure/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

As AI workloads scale across every industry, organizations need compute options that optimize throughput, latency, and unit economics across the AI lifecycle. To meet this demand, Oracle and Google Cloud are partnering to bring Google Tensor Processing Units (TPUs) to Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (OCI), making OCI the first hyperscaler outside Google to offer TPUs. Together, we will deploy TPU capacity on OCI starting in the calendar year 2027. TPUs: Co-designed from silicon to software Google built its first TPU more than ten years ago on a clear product thesis: co-designing custom silicon alongside optical networking, power delivery, liquid cooling, and compiler software yields gains in performance per watt and total cost of ownership that isolated components cannot match. TPU v1 delivered 30x the throughput per watt of contemporary processors. Today, TPUs train and serve Gemini across Google's billion-user products, and the eighth-generation TPUs deliver up to 12.

Source: Google Cloud Blog

Earlier this year, we introduced Gemini Robotics 2 — our next-generation intelligence layer enabling robots to think, act, and adapt in the physical world. At the core of this breakthrough is Gemini Robotics Embodied Reasoning 2 (ER 2) , a specialized Gemini model that serves as a robot’s high-level brain. Working across any robot embodiment — from stationary arms to full humanoids — Gemini Robotics ER 2 combines advanced spatial and temporal video understanding with long-horizon planning and built-in safety guardrails. This enables robots to converse naturally with humans, orchestrate low-level action models and hardware APIs, execute multi-minute tasks involving hundreds of decisions, and collaborate across multi-robot teams. Today, we are bringing this same frontier embodied reasoning model to enterprise production environments: Gemini Robotics ER 2 is now available in public preview on the Gemini platform.

### Announcing the Workload Conformance Working Group
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 04:30 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/27/announcing-workload-conformance-wg/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We are excited to announce that the Kubernetes community now has a Workload Conformance Working Group . The working group is writing a shared standard for how workloads behave at runtime on Kubernetes. To get it right, we need input from the people who build those workloads and from the people who run the clusters they're deployed on. Why workloads need a shared standard Certified Kubernetes tests the cluster, and Kubernetes AI Conformance tests whether a cluster can run AI and ML workloads. Nothing comparable exists for the workloads themselves, so cluster operators still check each application by hand before routine maintenance or a Kubernetes upgrade. The problems they find are familiar. A routine node drain is often how you discover that an application can't tolerate disruption. An upgrade can break an application that still depends on a removed API version . Others show up in day-to-day operation: workloads that ask for privileges they don't need, or reserve resources they never use. Kubernetes already documents much of this, including security and handling disruptions .

### Google Cloud and NASA: Testing robotic systems for deep space missions
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 04:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/public-sector/google-cloud-and-nasa-testing-robotic-systems-for-deep-space-missions/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

As NASA advances its Artemis program to send astronauts on increasingly difficult missions to explore more of the Moon, build a sustained human presence there with a Moon Base, and build on their foundation for the first crewed missions to Mars, crew safety and Earth-independent autonomy are paramount. An astronaut on a future deep space mission to Mars could face severe operational challenges—including round-trip communication latencies of up to 44 minutes, making real-time medical guidance for crew nearly impossible. Last year, Google Public Sector shared progress on the development of the Crew Medical Officer Digital Assistant (CMO-DA) - an automated Clinical Decision Support System (CDSS) designed to assist astronauts with real-time medical guidance in remote and isolated environments. Today, we are building on this innovation with the development of a proof-of-concept physical, robotic system that aims to support deep space missions and could assist astronauts during NASA missions.

### Making Failure Diagnosis Reliable with Pensive AI
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-26 17:04 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/making-failure-diagnosis-reliable-with-pensive-ai-6b0811a1c015?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

By Praneeth Yenugutala , Victor Dusa , Anastasia Samoilova , Megan Ren , Justin Wei , Marcelo Mayworm At Netflix, data and machine learning practitioners run millions of workloads each day across orchestration systems and compute engines such as Titus, Spark, Trino, and Druid. On peak days, roughly 100,000 workloads fail. When workloads fail, the failure itself is only the beginning. Someone still needs to find the useful signal in several logs, decide whether the cause sits in their code or the platform, and determine whether to retry, reconfigure, or escalate. Even a small share of difficult failures can consume engineering time, waste compute, and interrupt critical data pipelines. Our first answer to that problem was Pensive , an auto-diagnosis and remediation system in Netflix’s data platform. Pensive collected logs from failed workloads, used regular expressions and hand-authored Java rules to extract and classify errors, then passed retry or remediation guidance to products such as Maestro . It made ~ 50% of failures self-service and, in some cases, automatic.

### Beyond API Compatibility: Building a Tool to Measure the Blast Radius of Library Changes
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-23 18:32 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/beyond-api-compatibility-building-a-tool-to-measure-the-blast-radius-of-library-changes-9474f2fd4f90?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

By Roberto Perez Alcolea , Java Platform Team Quasar connects a proposed API change with observed references in consumer repositories, giving the producer evidence for a release decision At Netflix, shared Java libraries support applications developed across a large, independently evolving repository ecosystem. A library change begins in one repository, but its impact can reach many others. Producers can see the API diff in front of them. What they often cannot see is who still depends on the symbols that changed. The need for that visibility grows as coding agents take on more of the software development lifecycle. An agent can accelerate a local edit, but without cross-repository context it cannot judge downstream risk or help plan consumer migrations. The evidence a producer uses to assess a change can also guide the agent working on it. Consider a common case: removing a deprecated API. Deprecated entry points expand the surface producers must test, document, and support, and they can constrain how a library evolves.

### Single-tenant Titus: VM-level isolation on a multi-tenant container platform
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-22 22:19 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/single-tenant-titus-vm-level-isolation-on-a-multi-tenant-container-platform-6d8b1909d163?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Samartha Chandrashekar & Nick Parker You click play on a Friday night, and in the second that follows, dozens of services answer in milliseconds. Most of them run on Titus, our internal container platform. Titus is, in essence, a Kubernetes-based container hosting platform: we keep both the control plane and the data plane (the container hosts that actually run containers) patched and up to date, so application teams get a consistent and reliable container experience. They hand us a container, and we run it, with no host, cluster, or kernel to own. Titus launches millions of containers every week across tens of thousands of EC2 instances in multiple AWS regions. It is multi-tenant by default: many containers from many teams share each host. That density is what makes container compute efficient, and it is why Titus has been the default home for so much of Netflix’s compute for years. For most workloads, sharing a host works well. For a few, it does not, and those few include some of the most demanding real-time systems we run.

Authors: Divya Gadde , Ko-Jen Hsiao , Dhaval Patel and Moumita Bhattacharya The motivation for building the Netflix Foundation Model for personalization was laid out in this previous blog post. The Netflix homepage is powered by several specialized models and it requires significant time and resources to maintain and continue innovating on these individual models. Therefore, we decided to centralize member preference learning and have one powerful model learned from comprehensive user interaction histories and content data at a large scale and have this model distribute its learnings to other models. The previous two blog posts( 1 , 2 ) focus on the development of the Foundation Model and here we talk about how we integrate the Foundation Model into various applications. We found that there is extensive literature focused on the training and inference of large-scale transformer-based models. However, there remains a significant gap in practical guidance and research on how to effectively and efficiently integrate large models into existing production systems.

### Why Is My Kubernetes Job Still Pending? Tracing Kueue Admission with Headlamp
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-14 18:35 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/14/tracing-kueue-admission-with-headlamp/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

You submit a Kubernetes Job and wait for its Pods to appear. A minute passes. Then another. The Job is still suspended, there are no application logs, and there may not even be a Pod to inspect. In a cluster managed by Kueue , this can be completely normal. Kueue may still be deciding whether the Job can use the requested resources. The useful question is not only “Why is the Pod pending?” It is first “Has Kueue admitted the Workload?” The Kueue plugin for Headlamp brings that investigation into the Headlamp UI. Instead of reconstructing the admission path from several commands and large YAML objects, you can begin with the Workload, read its condition message, and follow links through the LocalQueue, ClusterQueue, ResourceFlavor, and Cohort. This article follows that Headlamp-first path. The command line appears only in a short optional section for reproducing the example. Before you begin You need a Kubernetes cluster with Kueue installed , a Headlamp installation , and the 0.1.0-alpha Kueue plugin .

### The Shift to cgroup v2 in Kubernetes: What You Need to Know
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-06 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/06/kubernetes-cgroups-v2-shift/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In Linux, cgroups (control groups) are a kernel feature used for managing system resources. Kubernetes uses cgroups to allocate resources like CPU and memory to containers, ensuring that applications run smoothly without interfering with each other. With the release of Kubernetes v1.31, support for v1 cgroup management moved into maintenance mode . Support for v2 cgroup management has been stable since Kubernetes v1.25. Compared with cgroup v1, cgroup v2 provides a single unified hierarchy, a more consistent interface, and a stronger foundation for resource isolation and modern resource-management features. Deprecation of cgroup v1 Kubernetes has deprecated cgroup v1 . Starting with Kubernetes v1.35, failCgroupV1 defaults to true , so the kubelet does not start on a cgroup v1 node by default. Administrators can temporarily set failCgroupV1: false in the kubelet configuration file , but removal will follow the Kubernetes deprecation policy . Further removal work is tracked in KEP-5573: Remove cgroup v1 support . If you are still on a release older than v1.

### Scaling Kubernetes Workloads with Node Swap
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-05 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/05/scaling-kubernetes-workloads-with-node-swap/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Memory is often the first hard limit a Kubernetes cluster hits. Nodes run out of RAM long before they run out of CPU, and the new wave of agentic AI workloads makes this worse. These workloads demand large memory footprints to start up and run untrusted code, then sit idle waiting for the next prompt. That idle but resident memory is expensive, and it caps how many pods a node can hold. This is where swap helps. Kubernetes support for running nodes with swap enabled reached General Availability in v1.34, and by backing that swap with fast NVMe solid state drives (SSDs), a node can page out dormant memory and pack in far more pods. This post explains how we benchmarked that approach across three workloads, including CI/CD kernel builds, sandboxed headless browsers, and isolated Python runtimes; we found density gains of up to 3×, often with little or no latency cost.

As Kubernetes adoption has grown, the conversation has shifted beyond running containers to managing increasingly complex application lifecycles. Modern platforms support stateless web services, stateful databases, batch processing, AI workloads, and platform services. At the same time, they must remain reliable during upgrades, scaling events, and infrastructure failures. Every Kubernetes user relies on SIG Apps, whether they realize it or not. Deployments, StatefulSets, DaemonSets, Jobs, and CronJobs form the foundation of how applications are deployed, updated, scaled, and operated across the Kubernetes ecosystem. SIG Apps is focused on improving workload resilience, refining application lifecycle management, and addressing the operational challenges that emerge when applications encounter node failures, rollout disruptions, and increasingly complex infrastructure environments.

### Kubernetes v1.37: Tracking When a PersistentVolumeClaim Was Last Used (Beta)
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-09-21 18:30 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/09/21/kubernetes-v1-37-pvc-last-used-time/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Kubernetes v1.37 promotes the PersistentVolumeClaimUnusedSinceTime feature gate to Beta (enabled by default). With this feature, the PersistentVolumeClaim (PVC) protection controller adds an Unused condition to each PVC, telling you whether any running pod currently references it — no custom tooling or cross-referencing required. For the API definition of PVC conditions, see the PersistentVolumeClaim API reference . Read on to learn how the Unused condition works and how to use it. Why track PVC usage? In large-scale Kubernetes clusters, it is common for users to create PVCs and then delete the associated pods without cleaning up the storage, because Kubernetes does not automatically delete PVCs when their pods are removed (to protect against accidental data loss). Over time, these orphaned PVCs may accumulate, silently consuming storage capacity and driving up cloud costs. Before Kubernetes v1.37, it was easy to identify an unused PersistentVolume, but much harder to determine whether a PVC was still being used.

### Will Fiber-Optic Cables Finally Get Made in Orbit?
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-26 12:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/zblan-fiber-optic-cables-iss
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In a lab in Luxembourg in 2024, engineers at Flawless Photonics unpacked a spool of glass fiber that had just returned from the International Space Station (ISS). As they threaded it onto their rewinding machine, the machine jammed—as happens sometimes—and they braced for the usual outcome: The fiber would snap. This time, though, nothing broke. It was an early hint that their experiment might have been successful. The Flawless Photonics team had set out to draw fibers of a heavy-metal fluoride glass called ZBLAN—named for its other components of zirconium, barium, lanthanum, aluminum, and sodium (chemical symbol Na)—in microgravity, aiming to achieve superior optical and physical properties. “We realized, wow, that fiber is much stronger than the stuff we had produced on Earth,” says Hubert Moser , the company’s cofounder and CTO. ZBLAN Fiber’s Microgravity Advantages Photonics engineers have been intrigued by ZBLAN for decades because it can, in theory, transmit light with 1 percent of the loss of silica.

Such visual reactive whole-body control remains challenging for humanoids. ### The Robot I Didn’t Expect to Build
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-19 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/agility-robotics-digit-5-redesign
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Agility Robotics , the company I cofounded out of my robotics lab at Oregon State University in 2015, has just launched Digit 5 . It’s designed to be the first humanoid robot that can safely and economically do useful work at scale, allowing us to bring tens of thousands of humanoid workers into the real world. Engineered to lift 23-kilogram bins onto high shelves and with a battery that can run for 90 minutes on a 9-minute charge, Digit 5’s goal is to handle heavy warehouse tasks shift after shift, filling the need for labor in jobs that are too physically stressful or too dull for humans to want to do. Digit 5 is what Agility has been working toward for over a decade. But the robot is heavy and bulky, lacking the graceful natural motion of its predecessors, and it’s farther than I thought possible from the robot I dreamed of building when I started this company. To deliver a useful commercial product, Agility has taken a path that is very different from what I first imagined.

### Hera Mission Prepares for Follow-Up Asteroid Imaging
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-18 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/esa-hera-dimorphos-rendezvous
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In one month, the European Space Agency’s Hera mission will make history. Moving at 12 kilometers per second relative to Earth, the mission is about to rendezvous with the binary Dimorphos-Didymos asteroid pair. NASA’s Double Asteroid Redirection Test (DART) mission slammed into Dimorphos in 2022, and now Hera will provide a follow-up view of the mission’s resulting handiwork. The European Space Agency (ESA) recently released a thought-provoking look at the perspectives of Hera and what an astronaut might see from the surface of the asteroids as the mission speeds by. But what would it be like to hitch a ride aboard Hera? Launched on a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral, Fla., on 7 October 2024, Hera is ESA’s first Space Safety Programme-class mission. The spacecraft took just over two years to reach its destination. Even now, the pair of asteroids is little more than a few pixels across in Hera’s view. The mission imaged Didymos in July 2026.

### Q&A: Mars Perseverance Rover Sets a Distance Record
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-13 15:02 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/mars-rover-distance-record
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

On 11 October , the Perseverance rover completed a drive that brought its total distance traveled on Mars to 28.10 miles (45.22 kilometers). This is a new record for Mars rovers, enabled in large part by Perseverance ’s ability to drive with much more autonomy than its predecessors. Over the past five years, Perseverance ’s autonomy software has been steadily upgraded, and to learn more about how the record was broken and what the future holds for the rover, we spoke with Mark Maimone , the Perseverance Robotic Operations Deputy Team Chief at NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory, who designed and developed the self-driving software for Mars rovers Spirit, Opportunity, Curiosity, and Perseverance. Before we talk about Perseverance, let’s pour one out for the previous record holder, Opportunity , which landed on Mars in January 2004 for a three-month mission and ended its epic journey 15 years later in February 2019.

### NASA Wants More Than 70 Moon Landings in the Next Decade—But How?
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-12 15:27 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-base-landings
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

At Firefly Aerospace in Texas, engineers have been doing what NASA’s chief, Jared Isaacman, likes to call “the near-impossible.” The Firefly team has been assembling Blue Ghost Mission 2 , a robotic spacecraft intended to land on the far side of the moon in 2027. No U.S. spacecraft has ever gone there. Not very many have landed on the near side, either, for that matter. Blue Ghost 2 has thousands of potential failure points, and there is almost no room for error. Two ships sent by a competitor, Houston-based Intuitive Machines , reached the moon’s near side in 2024 and 2025 but fell over on landing and returned little useful data. In 2024, another mission, Peregrine Mission One , by Pittsburgh-based Astrobotic Technology , missed the moon entirely because a single helium valve wouldn’t seat properly. In summary: Moon landings are supremely hard. So far, since Project Apollo ended in 1972, U.S. spacecraft have landed intact exactly…once.

### Lunar Mission Will Explore Old Lava Tubes as Potential Habitats
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-11 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-mission-gimli-artemis
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The chief aim of NASA’s Artemis Program is to create a “sustained program of lunar exploration and development.” Beyond returning astronauts to the moon for the first time since the Apollo era , NASA and other space agencies hope to build the infrastructure that will enable regular, long-duration missions to the lunar surface. In addition to targeting the South Pole-Aitken Basin , NASA is eyeing lunar caves as a potential site for habitats and other lunar facilities. Meanwhile, space agencies need to send large amounts of cargo and robotic explorers to the surface. This includes the Geophysical Instruments for Marius Lunar pit Investigation (GIMLI), a Planetary Science Institute –led proposal that will investigate a “skylight,” or a hole in an old lunar lava tube, to see if a large cave system extends beyond it . Such a location could provide a safe place for astronauts to operate on the surface in the future. The mission team will be led by Than Putzig , an associate director and senior scientist at PSI.

### This Disembodied Hand Is All the Robot You Need
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-08 12:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/walking-robotic-hand
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A hand attached to the end of an arm is a given for most humans, but what if robots didn’t have to play by the same rules as we do? Researchers from ETH Zurich have turned an off-the-shelf robotic hand into a completely self-contained robot that walks on its fingertips while using those same fingers to manipulate its environment. Besides making the perfect Halloween accessory for anyone planning to dress up as a member of the Addams Family—who were famously accompanied by a disembodied walking hand called Thing —this robotic hand could make it possible to operate controls and handle objects in places a traditional robot arm can’t reach, the researchers say. Soft Robotics Lab, ETH Zurich/YouTube Amirhossein Kazemipour , a Ph.D. candidate in the Soft Robotics Lab at ETH Zurich who led the project, believes this hand could prove useful in contexts like fiddly maintenance tasks in industrial settings, or in search and rescue, where a robot could detach its hand to explore confined spaces too tight for the rest of its body.

### Ukraine’s Air Defenses Have No Answer for Russia’s New Jet Drones
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-09-30 11:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/jet-powered-shahed-drone
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Since the discovery of the first remnants of a jet-powered Shahed drone near Kyiv in June 2025, these upgraded kamikaze UAVs have become a nightmare for Ukrainian civilians. Described by some analysts as the most significant Russian drone innovation since the beginning of the war, these uncrewed aerial vehicles are outrunning the beleaguered nation’s innovative interceptor systems, forcing a pivot in technology development and air-defense strategies. Russia manufactures the Iran-designed long-range Shahed drones , under the name Geran at its Yelabuga factory in Tatarstan, some 800 kilometers east of Moscow. In a little over a year, Russian engineers have developed three iterations of the jet-powered killer drone— Geran-3 , 4, and 5. Geran-3 , although fitted with a Chinese-made Telefly JT80 turbojet engine that enabled it to reach speeds of up to 370 kilometers per hour, was not very maneuverable and for that reason not much more effective than the propeller-powered Geran-2 .

### Generative AI Gives Spacecraft the Autonomy Engineers Once Feared
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-09-28 11:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/generative-ai-in-space-exploration
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Space was always supposed to be the final frontier of human exploration. It’s shaping up to be the final frontier for artificial intelligence too. Last December, NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory used Anthropic’s Claude models to help plan two Mars drives for the Perseverance rover , with human planners checking and adjusting the route before upload. In May, NASA and IBM put a compressed AI model on the International Space Station and a satellite to identify things like floods and clouds from orbit, the first model of its kind demonstrated in space. And in July, astronauts on the ISS tested a large language model to see if it could help with questions on maintenance procedures . These experiments point to a larger shift in space engineering. For decades, engineers on Earth determined what a machine in space would do, and the machine would do exactly that. Now, researchers are testing whether nondeterministic systems like generative AI can give spacecraft more flexibility to interpret their surroundings, plan tasks, and one day make decisions for themselves.

### Your Robot’s Safety Functions Already Work. What If the Input Lies?
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-27 16:14 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/ai-robot-safety-cybersecurity-vicone
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

This article is brought to you by VicOne . A mobile robot slows for someone in a hallway. A collaborative arm eases its movement as a worker approaches. A humanoid pauses to let a person pass. Each response depends on information about the robot or its surroundings: a distance reading, a position estimate, or a stop signal. But what happens when one of those inputs is wrong, and the robot still responds exactly as designed? The mobile robot reads the hallway as clear while someone is still there. The arm estimates that the worker is farther away. The humanoid does not receive a stop signal as a person crosses its path. In each case, a fault could cause that mismatch. So could deliberate manipulation . At VicOne LAB R7 , we examine how manipulated inputs can change robot behavior — and what that means for the systems meant to keep people safe. How an untrusted input can change a robot’s behavior A robot does more than measure its surroundings. It may interpret what it sees and hears to decide what to do next. Information placed in its environment can therefore influence its actions.

### I Used AI to Fake a Scientific Paper. Here’s What Happened
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-27 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/ai-generated-scientific-papers
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Science has a spam problem. And the spam filter is getting clogged up. According to all reports, academic publishing is drowning in what has come to be called AI slop: low-quality, mass-produced content generated with AI. By 2025, an estimated 57 percent of all published academic articles exhibited detectable evidence of large language model (LLM) influence, a massive jump from 12 percent in 2023. What’s more, over 12 percent of Nature Communications reviews were found to be completely AI-generated in 2025. Given that for every 40 papers showing statistical evidence of AI usage in 2025, only one formally disclosed it , these figures likely grossly underestimate LLM involvement today. Of course, evidence of AI use doesn’t necessarily mean a paper is slop. But some estimates suggest that hundreds of thousands of fake, often AI-generated, publications are now produced annually. “It’s not just the [scientific] record under threat, it’s science as a whole,” warns Amanda Sulicz , IEEE’s research-integrity manager.

### Build a Hidden Lock With a Magnetic Imager
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-26 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/magnetic
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Like many a nerd, I have a certain fascination with locks. This originates in reading the memoir Surely You’re Joking, Mr. Feynman as a teenager, in which Richard Feynman recounts picking locks and cracking combinations while at his day job working on the Manhattan Project at Los Alamos. I currently have a couple of locksport pick sets and training locks that I purchased at hacker conferences over the years (although I’m still terrible at it), and I delight in the YouTube exploits of folks like the LockPickingLawyer . All of which left me wondering if I could try building an interesting lock. One dodge that lockmakers have long used to challenge would-be plunderers is to conceal the existence of the lock itself. A modern variant of this is the magnetic lock, where a magnet is held or slid against an innocuous portion of a door or box, releasing a catch. The problem with this is that, once you guess the location of the lock, any strong magnet will open it.

### Renesas RX74M and RX74N – 600MHz industrial MCUs with RXv3e core, embedded MRAM, 22nm process
Source: CNX Software
Published: 2026-10-26 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cnx-software.com/2026/10/26/renesas-rx74m-and-rx74n-600mhz-industrial-mcus-with-rxv3e-core-embedded-mram-22nm-process/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Renesas has introduced the RX74M and RX74N, two new 32-bit RX family MCUs built on a 22nm process with embedded MRAM. Both MCUs integrate a 600MHz RXv3e core and are designed for industrial automation, motor control, robotics, and energy management applications. The MCUs feature up to 1MB of code MRAM, 1.5MB SRAM, and 256KB of zero-wait-state local RAM for real-time processing. The RX74M adds an integrated EtherCAT slave controller, dual Gigabit Ethernet MACs with TSN support, and an Ethernet switch for industrial networking, while the RX74N offers a single Gigabit Ethernet interface for general industrial applications. Other features include CAN FD, USB 2.0, I3C, high-resolution PWM timers, and hardware-based security with Renesas Protect Zone. Renesas RX74M and RX74N specifications: MCU – 32-bit RXv3e core clocked at 300/480/600MHz Up to 3,604 CoreMark at 600MHz Single- and double-precision floating-point support Trigonometric function accelerator Register bank save function for faster interrupt response 32KB [...

Deki Engine is an open-source modular C++ engine with a Unity-style visual editor for embedded devices, currently optimized for ESP32 platforms such as LilyGO T-Deck Plus, T-Display S3 AMOLED, and soon Elecrow CrowPanel Advanced 7″ ESP32-P4. The editor is available for Windows and Linux with a compiler for the 2D graphics engine, and everything is released under an Apache 2.0 license on GitHub. The project also works on desktop through SDL3, which may be convenient during development. Some of the highlights of the Deki Engine project: Workflow Build scenes visually (sprites, text, particles, tilemaps, state machines, tweens) Pick a board in the editor’s Build panel Flash it. The mini PC measures 114.8 x 76 x 31.3 mm and features two 4K HDMI outputs, Gigabit Ethernet, and M.2 expansion for NVMe storage or AI accelerators, along with an internal 40-pin GPIO header, camera interfaces, and a wide 5V to 20V DC input for industrial automation, digital signage, and IoT gateway applications. All I/O ports are placed on a single side to simplify cable management and stacking. Published: 2026-10-23 18:00 UTC

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Many engineers are more comfortable working behind the scenes developing technology than speaking to consumers about products. But Preethi Ashwath has learned to take center stage, talking about the “hearable” and wearable devices she has had a hand in developing as an analog integrated circuit designer in the consumer electronics division at Analog Devices of Wilmington, Mass. The IEEE member is one of the few women who work in the specialized field , where about 11 percent are female, according to career platform Zippia . Preethi Ashwath Employer Analog Devices, in Boston Title Integrated circuit designer Member grade Member Alma maters SJB Institute of Technology in Bengaluru, India; University of California, Irvine Ashwath stepped out from behind the scenes last year to meet with attendees at the Consumer Electronics Show (CES) in Las Vegas. She demonstrated Analog’s new earbuds, which track people’s heart and lung health through vibrations in their ear canals.

### Backdooring Open-Weight AI Models
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/backdooring-open-weight-ai-models.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

This is a problem: Any open model whose weights have been edited can carry a backdoor, whether it’s a task-specific fine-tune, a merged adapter or an abliterated build. We have abliterated models in the title because it’s the most popular reason people download modified weights without verifying what’s inside. Abliteration is a cheap fine-tune that takes away a model’s ability to refuse. You find the direction in the weights that carries “no” and subtract it. The model then answers things a safety-tuned version would turn down. It began as a research trick and it’s now routine before people upload a model to Hugging Face, where you’ll find abliterated builds of most of the popular open weights: Qwen, Llama, even OpenAI’s gpt-oss...

### Training AIs to Solve CAPTCHAs
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/training-ais-to-solve-captchas.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Interesting research : Abstract : Vision-language models (VLMs) can solve visual CAPTCHAs without task-specific training, but the agents built on them approach every challenge from scratch. For such an agent, the hundredth instance of a familiar puzzle costs as much time and compute as the first. Specialized detectors invert the trade-off, answering in milliseconds but only for categories they were trained on. Neither improves with exposure. We study what changes when a solver improves with use. Our system pairs a fine-tuned YOLOv8 detector with an open-weight VLM behind a confidence-based router, and runs entirely from screenshots and operating-system input events, with no browser automation or DOM access. It reaches 85.4% overall and 84.2% macro accuracy across 16 classes, exceeding either component alone. Every answer VLM produces also serves as a training label, so the detector absorbs categories it was never trained for, typically after one or two encounters and without human annotation. The same loop also repairs it.

### New Serious PHP Vulnerabilities Released and Patched
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-27 19:48 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/new-serious-php-vulnerabilities-released-and-patched.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

PHP maintainers have released fixes for two critical vulnerabilities: PHP 8.5.12, 8.4.27, 8.3.36 and 8.2.35 fix two critical vulnerabilities that can allow unauthenticated remote code execution. All PHP users are strongly encouraged to update immediately. Who is at risk: all PHP installations serving web requests, including PHP-FPM, Apache with mod_php, php-cgi, FrankenPHP and LiteSpeed. We consider the risk highest for mod_php, with a demonstrated exploit where an unauthenticated request can trigger the attack before any application code runs...

### New Records Obtained by EFF Expose More Problems with Medicare’s AI Experiment
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-27 19:05 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/new-records-obtained-eff-expose-more-problems-medicares-ai-experiment
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

New records obtained by EFF expose additional issues with Medicare’s AI-driven prior authorization program and show why we need stronger legal limits around government use of automated decision-making . EFF sued the government back in March for information about the Wasteful and Inappropriate Service Reduction (WISeR) model , a new Medicare program that uses AI to evaluate prior authorization requests for certain medical services. We previously released records showing that WISeR has resulted in delays and denials of care, operational chaos, and reports of patient harm. Today, we’re publishing over 800 new pages of records, including: Requests for information (RFIs) or requests for corrective action plans (CAPs) sent from the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) to four of the six WISeR vendors, along with the vendors’ responses. Applications of 66 companies to the WISeR model, including those of the 6 selected companies. Task orders and contracts for the evaluation, implementation, and monitoring of the WISeR model.

### French Court’s Site-Blocking Orders Drive Quad9 Out Of France
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-27 16:19 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/french-courts-site-blocking-orders-drive-quad9-out-france
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A nonprofit service that protects internet users’ privacy is leaving France. Quad9, a free DNS resolver, has said it will reluctantly stop serving users in France on November 24. This follows a French court order that Quad9 must block access to websites that are accused of infringing the copyrights of a sports broadcaster. Forcing a responsible privacy service out of an important market is a predictable result of trying to force infrastructure providers to be copyright police. When you visit a website, a DNS resolver reads the domain name you type in—like eff.org — and translates it into a numerical IP address that’s used to direct data across the internet. A DNS resolver can see the names of websites you connect to, so its privacy policies are important. Quad9 is an NGO that’s incorporated in Switzerland, in part to protect its users from data demands. The company also helps protect users from malware and phishing by blocking domains that are known to be malicious.

### Lessons Learned From 'AdFection' and What To Do Next
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-26 17:28 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/lessons-learned-adfection
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Last week, researchers at Citizen Lab revealed a horrifying new malware campaign, dubbed “AdFection.” It used an ad network to distribute spyware to journalist’s devices to infect them without the target ever needing to click anything. This sort of attack on journalists is dangerous and incredibly concerning, especially since it’s built on the backbone of a technology we already object to: behavioral advertising . Although this type of attack has only been described targeting journalists, you can take a few steps we outline in the next section to mitigate the risk no matter who you are. A few factors combined to make this attack a noteworthy first-of-its-kind. First, it utilized the surveillance ad-tech ecosystem to specially target the malicious code. Second, the spyware it delivered was so-called “zero-click,” meaning it required no interaction from the user to be infected: all that is required is for the ad to be delivered and code bundled along with it to be run, which happens automatically. Neither malvertising nor zero-click attacks are new on their own.

### Anti-Circumvention Laws Raise the Cost of Knowledge
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-26 17:20 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/anti-circumvention-laws-raise-cost-knowledge
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The vast amount of information on the open web requires researchers and journalists to adapt with new tools to make sense of it all. Checking facts, gauging public opinion, and other lines of inquiry depend on being able to analyze the overwhelming amount of material posted online every day. Unfortunately, responding to the AI industry’s voracious appetite for data, companies stewarding this public data are looking to cash in and block access to the open web. This makes the web harder to navigate, and threatens all kinds of important research. Reddit’s lawsuit against SerpAPI[1] and Perplexity threatens to do exactly that. Reddit was once a very open platform, fostering a community of alternative apps and extensions putting users in control of their experience. Since the rise of AI however, it changed tack. Over the last three years Reddit has closed APIs to users , limited UI features , and otherwise restrained access without login .

### The Vulnerability Apocalypse and Why We Should Fund Open Source
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-23 22:36 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/vulnerability-apocalypse-and-why-we-should-fund-open-source
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

If you use the internet at all, you may have noticed that a large percentage of sites suddenly went down earlier this week — and all your nerdiest friends were talking about "The Vulnerability Apocalypse” and this thing called PHP that powers nearly 70% of the websites on the internet . You may find yourself wondering: what is PHP, what just happened, what is the vulnerability apocalypse, and what do I need to do? To find out what you can personally do read the first blog post in this series! What Is PHP? PHP is a programming language used mainly to create interactive websites. Facebook, Wordpress, Wikipedia, Etsy, and other major sites all use or were originally built on PHP. It is a long-established mainstay of web development. But because it is open-source, maintenance of these critical systems depends on a small number of volunteers working on this in their free time. The disaster scenario that happened this past week is the perfect example of why we need more money and people supporting open-source projects. Corporations should be contributing both.

URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/computer-security-about-get-lot-worse-heres-what-you-can-do
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

It is a commonly held truth among computer geeks that there is no such thing as bug free software, or a secure computer. Any piece of software that anyone actually uses will have a security vulnerability somewhere in the code . The more complex that software is, the more likely there will be a vulnerability. Fortunately for everyone, finding severe vulnerabilities was generally considered an expensive and difficult task requiring years of training and deep knowledge of how computers and software worked. Until recently there was a steady background hum of vulnerabilities being found and patched, with major vulnerabilities that could cripple the entire internet only happening every few years. Now that is no longer the case. You may have noticed your nerdiest friends freaking out about the “Vulnerability Apocalypse” this week and waving their arms in panic about something called PHP. You may have also noticed that some of your favorite websites were down for a bit.
